  - Rise low 3250 to 3254.5 (`work/r6/x2-riselow.jpg`): the lower left is as before, pink mounds on a smooth dark field and a white lump at the bottom edge: NOT CURED. `pat.py` on 10 frames gives no pattern number (the window is longer); pop 1.01 at 3251.5 (limit 1.2), level step 0.13.
- Decision d253 (MAIL 796), frame 3012 at the draft tier, four stills (`out/work/jupiter/d253/`, `work/r6/d253.py`, picture `work/r6/d253-3012.jpg`), BEFORE the cure below:
  - full set, 3840 by 2160 scaled down against 1920 by 1080: mean difference 1.846, whole frame level +0.928.
  - web set, the same pair: 1.639, +0.893.
  - 1920 by 1080, web set against full set: 3.147, +0.607. 3840 by 2160 scaled down, web against full: 2.871, +0.571.
  - Seen in the difference pictures: between the sizes the storm's disc differs (its fine contrast is higher at 1920, largest low pass difference 4.98 levels at 873, 691); between the sets the globe's ground outside the plates differs as grain, inside the plates almost nothing.
- FAULT of mine against d253 (b), found by reading: `clouds.ts` handed the plates' texel size of the set in use (`uTexel`, `pixels[ctx.assets]`); the made detail under the plates starts where that size passes the pixel (`cut = uDetail.z * size` against `least`), so in the web set (plates of half the size) it started at twice the height. Cure: `uTexel` counts the full set's texels in both sets (`m.pixels.full`); the slope step of the stored heights, which has to be the set's own texel, reads `uNear.w` (`uPlateBoxNear.w` in `globe.ts`). The full set's numbers are the same by construction; proof by bytes below.
- (a) of d253 by reading: the terms of my stop that count screen pixels are `uPixel * uGrad` and the `dFdx`, `dFdy` gradients times `uGrad`, `own * turned / uFrame` (`uSteep`), `smear / uFrame` (`uTrust`), `parent * uFrame` (`uPatchFade`), `vfoot * uFrame` and `uWispShadow.z * uFrame` in the globe, `radiusPx / frame` in `set.ts`. The plates exchange by place (`uJoin` on the ground), not by pixels. Not through the factor: `fwidth(rt)` at the globe's edge (`globe.ts` 290) and in `io.ts` 72, 79, an edge of one pixel at either size.

## 23:36 UTC: MAIL 807 (word for word), the deadline of 00:10 is lifted

MAIL 807 from the Builder (/root) to jupiter_coder, "the ruling on your answer to MAIL 776 (files pair-2895, oval-zoom, x1-dive, x1-rise-b). Build on it; name when the conditions below are met, not at 00:10 as it stands.

1. The place stands: the eye of the pale oval at lon -3.19, lat -15.2. The storm may show in the hold frame and on the way down. Brandon asked for a place on the planet that the camera goes to, and a storm that is there from the hold on is that. Decision d254; Neptune gets the same ruling in the same words.

2. Four conditions, each a cause and none a weight. Your two doubts are the first two of them and I share both: at 6 times the storm in the hold reads as a hole with a black side and sharp rings, and at 1 to 1 as a dark dot beside the moon that the approved hero does not have.
(a) one Sun, the globe's, and one fill. In x1-dive at 3030 and 3050 the shaded cloud of the storm is dark blue grey and still readable; in the hold at 2895 the same side is near black. So the hold draws that shade darker than the close field itself does. Find where (shade taken twice, once by the globe's light and once baked in the storm, or a fill of the sky that the close field has and the far level lacks) and cure it there. The proof is one curve in the NAMED mail: the darkest 5 by 5 mean and the brightest 5 by 5 mean inside the storm, frame by frame from 2895 to T, with no step and nothing in the hold darker than the same storm at 3030.
(b) the storm goes through the globe's own filter by the pixel's foot (the frame law of MAIL 796): in the hold nothing in it is crisper than the hero's own detail round it. The rings are finer than anything the hero has at that scale; they are read below the pixel's foot. Proof: the mean gradient inside the changed box at 2895 beside the mean gradient of the Great Red Spot's rim in the same frame.
(c) no light of its own and no law by height or by the clock, as ruled in MAIL 776.
(d) its tones at the hold stay inside what the hero has within about 200 px of it (its belts, its ovals, the Great Red Spot), unless the field gives another tone at every height, which the curve of (a) shows.

3. Past that you do not weaken it or make it smaller. Its size follows from the horizon of the approved close image, as decided before. The jury sees it as it stands. If R1 marks the storm in the hold in two rounds, the plateau rule moves the place onto a storm the hero already has.

4. With the NAMED mail: the pair of 2895 at 1 to 1 and at 6 times again, the curve, the two gradients, x1-dive and x1-rise at the frames of your sheets, handover and smooth results of your own run, and the range patch proof asked in MAIL 796. Say the time you will name by in one line when you have read this."

CAUSE: it is the horizon haze painted in the surface view's sky plate (`sky-terrain.webp`). The plate is a picture at infinity, cut below the surface view's own horizon circle (`skyFloor`, dip 0.0892 radians), so from a higher camera its haze floats above the true limb by the dip of the limb at that height less 5.1 degrees. It is not a share by height (the sky share is 1 in every frame with sky), it is air drawn at infinity. CURE (not in this commit, it changes the judged 3150 and the proofs would start again): take the air out of the plate (the plate keeps stars and the Milky Way only) and let the deck's limb table carry all air over the limb by tangent height, measured again on the approved image by `deck/limb.py`. First work after naming, together with the rise's lower left.

## 01:42 UTC (25 Nov): the rings' cause, how the sky plate is cut, what waits for the chain (read after the section above)

- Rings arriving in place 2988 to 2994 round the storm, out to about 60 units (`work/r8/c1-rings.png`). CAUSE: the patches hold the deck's plates laid back over the far plate's whole footprint, the wrap (the hero's level) holds the storm only inside the small disc's border (`deck.json` `plates.upward.border` [13.0, 16.5] units, ruling 1 (a) of MAIL 776), so the exchange from the wrap to patch-wide (`patches.json` `fade` [1.02, 1.5] parent texels per pixel) brings a ring system the hero does not have. Three ways, for the Builder to rule: (1) lay the rings back into the wrap (the Builder objected to rings in the hold, MAIL 807 2 (b)); (2) end the ring system at the small disc's border in the patches and the plates too, so every level holds the same ground outside the disc (the surface view sees ground beyond 16.5 units of the vortex only in the last 1 to 7 px under the horizon: camera about 4.4 units from the vortex and 1.08 units high, radius about 270 units, horizon at 24 units; the dive's last frames would lose the relief beyond the disc); (3) leave. A wider exchange is only a softer bridge and is not offered.
- How the surface view's sky plate is cut in the dive and the rise: `place.json` `handover.dive.skyFloor` and `handover.rise.skyFloor` true (my own option), `skyFrames` 0; the engine's `floored(patch, skyFloor)` (`src/core/engine.ts`) gives the plate a ring from `floorRing(floor, patch.tan, config.raw.handover.skyFloor)` (`src/core/skyfloor.ts`; shared numbers `handover.skyFloor` marginDeg 0.25, featherDeg 4): a disc in the plane of the plate that hides what lies under the horizon of the surface view at the seam. So the plate above that horizon is whole at every height of the dive, and its painted haze with it: the floating band of the entry above.
- The approved image inside the plate: horizon at rows 48 (column 200), 252 (column 960), 499 (column 1700) of 1080; sky share 0.246; mean level of the sky 30.0. The plate (`sky.json` terrain, vfov 64 degrees, margin 0.125, gain 5.5) was made from `sky-lin.npy` (the approved image's sky in linear light with the margins of `sky/margins.py`), so everything the approved image has above its horizon is in it, air and stars alike.

- What P1 has to keep in mind (read from the reader and the shader, 06:20 to 06:36 UTC):
  - The reader's pattern is 1 minus a correlation, so a veil that only lowers the ground's contrast costs nothing while the contrast stays over 1 level. What costs is a pattern of the veil's own that does not ride with the ground: the table's step from 0.12 to 1.0 between sines 0.063 and 0.2 is about 80 px tall at 3.8 units of height (13 to 91 px under the horizon) and its own band pass answer (8 to 40 px) is a level stripe along the limb that sweeps over the ground while the camera sinks.
  - Geometry for it: sine of the ray at the ground is about h/d minus d/(2R), the angle under the level h/d plus d/(2R), the horizon's dip sqrt(2h/R), about 1000 px a radian. Ground 34 units out stands at sine 0.20 from 8.6 units of height (3029) and at 0.061 from 3.8 (3037).
  - So a law that holds: a veil whose change over the screen is slow against 40 px wherever the ground under it still shows over 1 level, or one that lies wholly within the 20 px at the limb the reader does not judge. And `through` by sine alone veils the side away from the Sun as well, where the approved image holds dark bands up to the limb: a share by sine and azimuth (as `added` has) serves the match too.

## 25 Nov, 06:39 to 07:12 UTC: third named commit of round 2, the air's law

- The NAMED mail of `b79d375e` went out 06:39 UTC. MAIL 950 (06:39): it does not go into take batch 10 (pattern line further from the limit than main: dive 1.24 against 0.73, rise 1.26 against 0.64 to 0.69; a level line at 3287 + 1/2 that main does not have; 3150 at 7.358 against 6.578 of round 46's pack). Third named commit due 08:09 UTC, goes into the round as it stands. Order: (a) the air as a law of the path's length through one layer of a fixed thickness, (b) the level line at 3287 + 1/2, (c) the lower left of the rise 3251 to 3264, (d) the match of 3150, (e) item 3.
- How the experiments were read: still pairs at draft tier, 3029 against 3037 (dive) and 3252 against 3260 (rise), with `work/r11/tmpair.py`: the reader's own `pattern` (tools/img/smooth.py) at quarter size over the ground's true map from the director's cameras. Two numbers a pair, each the 99th percentile: read on the earlier frame with the later one carried back from its finer picture, and read on the later frame. These are not the numbers of `npm run smooth` (the named commit read 1.24 there where this reading says 1.81); they rank variants, nothing more.
- What the pattern counts. It takes the detail between 8 and 40 px, divides by the local contrast and judges only where both frames have more than 1 level of it. A veil that only lowers contrast costs nothing. Two things cost: where the share that comes through is low (0.14 and under) the ground's detail falls to about 1 level and what is left is not the ground's pattern; and the tables' own profile at the lowest sines. At frame 3037 every sine under 0.056 lies within 10 px of the limb, and those 10 px are the ground between 35 and 50 units from the camera, which frame 3029 shows 110 px high. So the lower 15 of the 24 knots, drawn in 10 px, are laid over 110 px of the earlier frame as a pattern of their own.
- The readings (dive earlier, dive later, rise earlier, rise later; then 3150 against the approved image, drafts at full size, `work/t/cmp.py`):
  - as named (`b79d375e`): 1.81, 1.22, 0.71, 1.47; 7.44
  - air off: 0.64, 0.12, 0.69, 0.58; 9.42 (final tier)
  - share as named, no added light: 1.44, 0.60, 0.71, 1.70
  - added light as named, every ray through whole: 1.82, 0.83, 0.71, 0.53
  - as named, both tables held flat from sine 0.063 down: 1.50, 0.89 (dive only)
  - at least 0.7 comes through, added light refit, not flat: 1.09, 0.42, 0.71, 0.77; 8.42
  - slab 0.014 a unit, flat from sine 0.05 down (the law taken): 0.64, 0.33, 0.71, 0.58; 8.40
  - slab 0.0516 a unit, clear 0.1 to 0.4, flat from 0.05 down: 0.86, 0.70 (dive); 7.93
  - The rise's earlier reading (0.69 to 0.71 in every variant) is the lower left corner, the plates' paint; the air does not touch it. The dive's 0.64 is there with no air at all: the far ground squeezed at the later frame's horizon.
- The law of the path's length, weighed as the Builder wrote it. For an eye above the layer the path through a layer of thickness T is a function of the sine at the ground point alone, -R s + sqrt(R^2 s^2 + 2 R T + T^2). So with T equal to the eye's height of the approved view (0.978 units, the lowest the eye ever stands: the flight starts 0.32 above it) a table by the path is the table by the sine at every frame, and nothing changes. A thicker layer gives the same table back at 3150 but veils more from above (a ray at sine s reads the table at s times 0.978 / T), and every ground point's sine falls by the same ratio a frame at any T (0.44 over the 8 frames 3029 to 3037), so the step of the table still crosses a ground point in the same number of frames. What cures the crossing is not the thickness but a table without a step: the plain slab, exp(-k times the path), falls from 0.92 to 0.82 over the whole table. The measured share cannot be given back by any slab (it is 0.98 at sine 0.159 and 0.19 at 0.063, then 0): it is not a share of light, it is how little of my plates' far field the approved image shows.
- Taken: `deck.json` `air.layer` { per 0.014, flat 0.05 }, `assets-src/jupiter/deck/air.py` writes the share as exp(-per times path) for a layer as thick as `view.eye` stands high, the added light as the approved view's cell means over that share of the plates' means, both held from the first knot at or over `flat` downwards; `clear` unchanged. No shader change, the table's form is the same. Bake: `python3 assets-src/jupiter/deck/air.py` (37 s; share 0.825 at the horizon, added toward the Sun 1.52 green, away 0).
- What it costs, known: at 3150 the far ground on the side away from the Sun stays lit (0.82 of the plates) where the approved image is dark blue, because added light cannot darken. The match of 3150 on drafts at full size goes from 7.44 to 8.40. The dark far side cannot come from the air without the ground changing under the sinking camera (every variant with a low share reads red in proportion); it would have to be on the ground itself, by place, and that changes the hold. Not done.
- Not done in this commit: (b) the rise's turn, (c) the rise's lower left, (e) item 3.

## 25 Nov, 08:19 UTC: MAIL 972 (Builder, high, word for word): the ruling on the third named commit, the way for round 3 of 3 (read this first after a compaction)

"MAIL 972 from the Builder (/root), 08:19 UTC. To jupiter_coder. Answer to your NAMED db67666d10184720af679c613d3ab4d13bbbdda9 and the ruling you ask.

2. RULED on your two ways. Neither, as they stand.
(two) The dark far side laid on the ground by place: refused. I looked at the hold pair at 2895: the storm sits on the lit face, far from the terminator. A darkening pinned to the ground would stand there as a dark region on the lit face of the globe. One body means the hold may show the place, but not a shade the Sun does not cast there.
(one) The dial per 0.0516 with clear [0.1, 0.4]: refused. It buys the match (7.58) with the dive (0.86, red). A dial that comes with the camera's height is the thing the frame law forbids (d251, d253), and the dive's green of this commit is kept.

3. The way I ask for round 3 of 3, by cause. In the approved close image the right third is dark blue because of the ray, not because of the place: (a) the air's own light goes by the angle between the ray and the Sun (strong forward near the Sun, weak to the side and behind), and (b) the loss along the ray goes by its path through the air (long for a grazing ray to far ground, short for a steep ray from orbit), while (c) the deck's own light goes by the Sun's true height over the place. All three are functions of the ray, the place and the light. None is a function of the clock or of the camera's height as such. From orbit at 2895 the ray meets the lit face steeply, so the hold should move only near the limb, where a long path is true.

4. Before you build it, measure and send: the Sun's height over the local level at the place at 3150 by the scene; the mean colour of the approved right third against yours; and how long the path through your slab is for a ray to the far ground of the right third at 3150 and for the ray to the same place at 2895. If the scene's Sun stands high over the place while the approved image has it low, say so with the numbers: then the dark side cannot come from (c) and has to come from (a) and (b) alone, and I want to see that before a commit.

5. Proof I need with the next NAMED: pairs at 2895 (with the hash of the judged hold against db67666d and what moved), at 3150 against the approved with the match by thirds, the dive 3019 to 3035 and the rise 3240 to 3265 by your own slices. Not laid if the hold gets worse or the dive goes red again.

6. The rise red at 3252 stays your first item beside this: say by frame and cause what changes in place there (the slab's far edge, the deck's level exchange, or the air).

Next state mail by 09:50 UTC."

- Cause of the corner: the banks' shadows on the ground were cut off by the footprint of the pixel (the whole bank was multiplied by a fade over 0.096 to 0.288 units of the footprint's long axis). The footprint goes with the camera, so the far end of the shadows grew over the far ground as the eye rose. A shadow is on the ground whoever looks at it. This is item 3's question too: the fade by footprint took away shadows the eye would resolve, their streaks are wide across the line of sight.
- Item 1, as it stands in the tree (`v3`): `deck.life.haze.thick` = units 0.1, taps 4, spread 0.2144 (the standard deviation of one octave of the value noise, sqrt((26/35)^2 / 12)). `layered` returns the mean and the share of variance its faded octaves lost. `hazeOne` fades each octave by the larger of the pixel's footprint and the run of the ray through the slab over the octave's length (`octaveFade`), and takes the cover as a smoothstep about the middle of `cover` with the half width 0.5 * sqrt((cover.y - cover.x)^2 + 20 * spread^2 * lost): a smoothstep of half width a is the distribution function of a bell whose variance is a^2 / 5, so adding a variance v widens the full width by 20 v under the root. `hazeAt` takes 4 taps along the run. The lit sheet's run is the ray's way between the spheres at the band's height plus and minus half the thickness; the shadow's run is the Sun's way through the same slab. The kill of the whole bank by the footprint is gone (`footprint` removed from `deck.json`). Neither the eye's height nor the clock enters, only the ray and the pixel's footprint.

The plates are another picture of the same ground: the repaint of 23 Nov put a ring of pale billows on a smooth floor round the funnel, the canvas has dark aprons with fine detail there. The tone field darkens the plates beside the canvas, so the floor reads as a smooth dark blue grey field, and the line cuts the billows.
- On the ground (`work/r14/wide.py`, picture `work/r14/w1-comp-lines.jpg`): the fit camera is turned, so the canvas' left border runs from the corner near (-0.5, -3.6) to the upper left ((-5.1, 0), (-10.1, 4)) and its bottom border from the corner to the right ((4.4, -3.5), (12.75, -2)). The cure being built (`work/r14/beyond/`, to become `assets-src/jupiter/plates/beyond.py`): the ground outside the canvas' borders in the fore and the near plate and in their height maps is made from the canvas' own ground laid back from above. Pieces of that ground (colour and height together, unturned, taken from inside the borders but not from the funnel) are set outward from the border, each chosen by least difference to what stands already and joined to it along a cut of least difference (image quilting; seeded, so a second run gives the same file). The plates are written over tone and calm (stored value = wanted value over tone times calm), so both fields stay as they are. Not touched: canvas, chart, the heights inside the borders, tone, calm, the globe's levels (no `upward.py`), so 3150 and the hold are to keep their bytes.

THE TRUE CAUSE OF 3150'S NUMBER (12:30 to 12:50 UTC): the ground of the judged frame stood 1.41 px right and 0.44 px down of the approved image's ground (`work/r14/shift.py`: 1.15 px in the middle columns, 1.8 at the sides; the sky at 0.00), the same in every final since 24 Nov. Two causes, both measured:

- The film's camera drift (`src/director/film.ts`, `pose.quaternion.multiply(drift(time))`; `drift` in `src/director/rig.ts`) turns the camera at 3150 + 1/4 by pitch -8.98e-5, yaw -9.87e-4, roll -2.87e-4 rad: 0.853 px of yaw at the 64 degree lens (`out/work/tmp/drift.ts`).
- The lay back counts each pixel of the picture at its upper left corner (`geom.rays`; the shader's `spot = at + vec2(0.5, -0.5)` in `seenAt` follows it), so the picture's middle lies half a canvas pixel right and up of the lens's line: 0.34 film px each way.
- Canvas, core and public core lie on the approved image at 0 px; the storm's turn, the shutter and the eye's place were ruled out one by one.

The cure (`plan.ts`, `terrain()`; `deck.json`, `flight.aimPx` [-0.5, 0.5]): the flight's camera gets one fixed turn, the half pixel times the inverse of the drift at the judged instant (`drift` is read from `src/director/rig`, nothing in `src/director` is touched). The film's camera at 3150 + 1/4 is then the camera of the lay back to the pixel; the flight's motion and the drift's own motion stay what they were; every frame from 2970 to 3314 moves by about 1 px; the hold is not touched.

- THE WEDGE, third way (`work/r14/beyond/prep.py`, `syn.py`, `apply.py`; pictures `s1-wide.jpg`, `s2-wide.jpg`, `s2-corner.jpg`, `s1-src.jpg`):
  - Source: the canvas' plain ground only, 3.2 square units south of the eye: inside the borders, 0.9 to 3.5 units from the point under the fit camera (sharp from above), more than 2.2 units from the storm's centre, towers (the tallest 18 % by height over its 0.3 unit mean) and the blue glints cut out with a margin.
  - What is laid: only the detail of that ground under 0.2 units (the log of the colour less its own 0.2 unit mean, and the height less its mean), never its tone. Each copy is the source turned about the storm's centre and scaled along the radius (96 copies: every 8 degrees, scales 1, 0.82, 0.67, 0.55), so the grain keeps its angle to the flow at the new place. No copy lies nearer than 1 unit to its source. At each point the copies are weighed by a seeded, ground fixed noise (0.4 units) and summed with the sum of squares kept at 1, so the borders between copies are soft and irregular and no tile shows.
  - Tone: the canvas' own 0.2 unit mean at the nearest point of the border, carried outward and given back to the plates' own tone with the distance from the border (0.5 to 1.8 units) and with the distance from the point under the fit camera (4.8 to 6.8 units, where the canvas itself ends). The plates' own detail returns with the same weight. Heights go the same way.
  - Under the canvas' own soft edge (100 to 200 canvas pixels inside the border) the plate is set to the canvas, so the edge mixes the canvas with itself.
  - Written into the fore and near plate and their height maps (full and web), each coarser one the finer one filtered; the globe's levels follow by `upward.py`.
